市場焦點
美國
• 10月S&P Global綜合PMI初值由58.4降至57.6，雖較前月回落，但仍處高檔區間；服務業動
能略為放緩，製造業PMI則升至56.0，創近4年半新高。新訂單增速加快，顯示企業需求、投
資及庫存回補動能仍具韌性，然而企業售價與投入成本同步上升，反映需求改善、供應鏈延
遲及成本轉嫁效應持續存在，通膨降溫進程仍具不確定性。整體而言，市場關注焦點轉向通
膨與利率走勢，高利率環境恐維持較長時間，進而推高股債資產評價波動。
中國
• 中國國家統計局公布第三季GDP年增率4.5%，優於市場預期，在出口與科技製造業帶動下，
短期成長可望維持穩定，但若內需未能有效改善，經濟成長動能恐難全面擴散，建議聚焦在
半導體、AI硬體、伺服器供應鏈，及高端製造相關產業，至於消費、房地產及傳統內需類股，

仍需觀察後續刺激政策與消費信心回升情況。
法國
政局動盪
• 法國金融市場因各地抗議活動持續擴大，面臨股債雙跌的局面。市場憂心法國政府可能擴大支
出以平息抗議，加劇財政可持續性疑慮。法國10年期公債殖利率一度突破5%，法德利差亦擴
大至歐債危機以來高點。不過，歐洲央行系統性壓力綜合指標仍處於歷史相對低檔，遠低於歐
元區主權債務危機期間，短期內演變為新一輪主權債務危機的風險仍相對有限。

美國PMI維持強勁擴張，經濟成長續航但通膨壓力再升溫
• 企業需求強勁支撐美國景氣維持高檔擴張：美國10月綜合PMI初值由58.4降至57.6，雖略低於前月，但仍處於近五年來
的高位區間，顯示企業活動持續高速擴張；其中服務業PMI為57.9、製造業PMI升至56.0並創53個月新高，新訂單增速
更創逾4年半最佳表現，反映內需穩健、出口需求改善與企業補庫存效應同步發酵，美國第四季經濟動能仍具韌性。
• 成本上升與供應瓶頸推升通膨風險：企業售價漲幅創2022年8月以來最高，能源、運輸、金屬原料及薪資成本同步上揚，
加上供應鏈交期延長與訂單積壓擴大，使企業得以持續轉嫁成本，暗示未來數月消費者通膨可能再度偏離聯準會目標區
間，市場對利率維持較高水準的預期恐因此升溫。
• 後市觀察：景氣韌性有利獲利成長，但通膨可能推升殖利率、壓抑高評價資產。投資配置建議優先布局美國大型科技股、

美國金融股及工業資本支出受惠族群，可透過NASDAQ 100 ETF、S&P 500 ETF及美國銀行股ETF參與企業獲利成長；
固定收益則以短天期投資級債為主，以降低利率波動風險。
資料來源：Bloomberg， 2026.10.24
S&P Global PMI 走勢

海外資金流入美國市場，支撐美國資產吸引力
• 海外資金持續流入美國：美國財政部8月TIC資料顯示，
整體資金淨流入1,998億美元，其中民間部門淨流入
1,785億美元，為主要資金來源；外資淨買進美國長
期證券3,007億美元，遠高於美國投資人加碼海外資
產216億美元，反映全球資金在經濟成長與流動性考
量下，對美國金融市場仍維持高度的配置需求。
• 全球資金偏好美國資產：外資8月淨買入美國股票
1,319億美元，高於美國公債與公司債，顯示海外投
資人仍看好美國企業獲利與經濟基本面，雖然短期國
庫券持有量下降，部分跨境美元資金亦出現波動，但
以10月近況來看長天期公債標售仍吸引境外央行、
保險資金及大型資產管理機構參與，反映高殖利率水
準對長期資金仍具吸引力。在經濟成長維持韌性與企

業獲利支撐下，美國資產仍為全球資金配置的重要核
心市場。
• 市場看法：海外資金流入有助反映美國資產吸引力，
惟後續仍須觀察通膨、Fed政策及企業獲利表現。投
資布局建議可以美國投資等級債、美國中短天期公債
及大型AI基礎建設受惠族群為核心配置，兼顧收益、
防禦及長期成長潛力。

顧
歷史數據顯示全球股市突破歷史新高後，後續上漲機率高
• 很多人直覺以為見高就是頂，但過去20年的統計資料顯示，全球股市在創下新高後，多數時間選擇繼續向上走。
• 勝率顯著偏高：全球股市(MSCI全球指數)創新高後持有一年，獲得正報酬的機率高達62%，三個月後的正報酬機率更達
71%。美股與台股表現最亮眼，標普500指數創高後一年上漲機率為76%，那斯達克為79%；而台灣股市表現更為驚人，創
高後三個月內上漲的機率高達82%。
• 下檔拉回風險有限：在創高後短期(一個月內)修正幅度其實相當溫和，全球多數主要市場的回徹幅度中位數僅1%～2%左右。
• 突破前高優於連續創新高：若市場在歷經至少一個月以上的整理後突破前高，其未來一年的平均報酬率高達8.4%，遠高於
連續每天創新高的3.9%。這代表市場經適度整理後的突破，具備更健康的上漲動能。

創新高後的平均遠期報酬率
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.22

顧
市場寬度收窄，由少數巨頭獨撐
• 當前的市場特徵由少數巨頭帶頭衝：科技股權重較高的指數(如那斯達克、台股、韓股)，長期以來都有少數權重股拉動指數
的現象。自2024年以來，全球13個主要市場中有10個市場在創新高時，參與上漲的股票比例明顯下降。已開發市場創新高
時高於200日均線的股票比例從過去75.5%降至65.1%，新興市場更降至61.3%。這意味著指數看似風光，但多數個股並未
同步創高，當漲勢過度集中於少數大型科技巨頭時，整體市場的抗風險能力會相對減弱。
• 小結與投資建議：歷史經驗顯示，創新高本身是強勢市場的特徵，後續繼續走揚的機率遠大於立刻崩盤。然而，鑑於目前市
場寬度收窄、權重高度集中於科技股的現狀，盲目追高個股的風險已顯著增加。投資建議可以利用ETF掌握大盤趨勢，由於

指數上漲主要由龍頭股驅動，透過市值型ETF(如追蹤標普500ETF)能直接享受龍頭巨頭帶來的指數創高紅利，避免選錯個股
而賺了指數、賠了價差。此外，提防短期股市回檔，可運用定期定額分批布局或等待大盤適度拉回整理再行加碼，降低追高
風險。
創新高時不同水準的市場寬度佔比
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.22
已開發市場和新興市場創新高時的寬度

顧
法國政局動盪加劇財政壓力，股債同步承壓
• 法國政局動盪加劇財政壓力，股債同步承壓：法國各地抗議活動持續擴大，最初由學生發起，隨後因生活成本高漲而引發其
他群體響應。在財政空間有限、俄烏戰爭與美伊衝突推升能源價格的背景下，政府難以有效緩解民生成本壓力，法國金融市
場因此面臨股債雙跌的局面。
• 公債殖利率攀升，但系統性風險仍相對可控：市場憂心法國政府可能擴大支出以平息抗議，加劇財政可持續性疑慮。法國10
年期公債殖利率一度突破5%，法德利差亦擴大至歐債危機以來高點。不過，歐洲央行系統性壓力綜合指標（CISS）仍處於
歷史相對低檔，遠低於歐元區主權債務危機期間，顯示金融市場尚未出現廣泛的系統性壓力，短期內演變為新一輪主權債務
危機的風險仍相對有限。
G7社會動盪指數，法國高居榜首

資料來源： Beco models country，2026.10.19 資料來源： Bloomberg，2026.10.19

資料來源： Bloomberg，2026.10.15 資料來源： tradingeconomics，2026.10.21
日本貿易收支
• 日本貿易逆差擴大，進口增幅高於出口：日本2026年9月貿易逆差擴大至5,366億日圓，較去年同期的2,777億日圓增加93%，
高於市場預期的1,810億日圓，連續第五個月呈現貿易赤字。出口年增16.7%至10.97兆日圓，受惠於半導體相關電子零件、
製造設備及汽車出口成長；惟進口年增18.9%至11.51兆日圓，增幅高於出口，主要受到原油、半導體電子零件及液化天然
氣進口增加帶動。其中，原油進口年增51.1%，部分反映日本在荷姆茲海峽局勢緊張下，積極分散能源採購來源。
• 日本央行本週料維持利率不變：日本央行9月將政策利率調升25個基點至1.25%，創31年來新高。考量前幾次升息對經濟的

傳導效果仍待觀察，加上貿易逆差擴大反映進口成本壓力，多數分析師預期日本央行本週將暫停升息，以評估貨幣緊縮對經
濟與通膨的影響。

中國國家統計局公布第三季GDP年增率4.5%，優於市場預期
• 中國國家統計局公布第三季GDP年增率4.5%，優於市場預期：顯示中國經濟在第二季落底後呈現溫和回升，從季度表現來看，
一、二、三季增速分別為5.0%、4.3%及4.5%，反映政策支持與外需復甦開始發揮效果，全年達成官方4.5%成長目標的機率
明顯提升，第三季經濟改善的主要動能來自出口，受惠於全球企業持續擴建AI基礎設施，及科技產品需求強勁，中國高科技
產品出口表現亮眼，尤其是積體電路、伺服器、電腦零組件，及儲存設備等產品增幅顯著，高技術製造業維持雙位數成長。
同時，中國對美國及歐盟出口維持強勁，推升貿易順差走升，為支撐經濟的重要力量。然而，內需疲弱仍是當前經濟最大隱
憂，房地產調整尚未結束，居民消費與企業投資意願保守，使經濟復甦結構呈現「外熱內冷」的特徵。

• 展望後市：在出口與科技製造業帶動下，短期成長可望維持穩定，但若內需未能有效改善，經濟成長動能恐難全面擴散，建
議聚焦在半導體、AI硬體、伺服器供應鏈，及高端製造相關產業，至於消費、房地產及傳統內需類股，仍需觀察後續刺激政
策與消費信心回升情況。
資料來源：開源證券，2026.10.22

• 彭博預估騰訊、阿里巴巴在中國AI競爭中勝出機率較高：彭博產業研究認為，中國AI產業未來將由大型科技平台主導，其中騰
訊、字節跳動與阿里巴巴憑藉龐大用戶基礎、數據資源、資金實力及完整生態系統，在AI競賽中具備明顯優勢，這些企業可將
AI廣泛應用於電商、社群、遊戲、金融與雲端業務，提升營運效率並強化競爭門檻，例如：阿里巴巴利用 AI 驅動電商平台上的
個人化產品推薦，而騰訊則運用 AI 優化短影音廣告並強化微信功能。騰訊自 2026 年 7 月起開始將混元 AI 模型整合至其電子
遊戲中，使遊戲角色及助理能更準確地回應玩家，然而，AI模型訓練與基礎建設投入龐大，獲利能力短期仍受壓抑，且須面對
監管、資料隱私及晶片供應等挑戰。此外，智譜、月之暗面、MiniMax及商湯等AI新創雖具技術創新能力，但缺乏規模效益、

穩定現金流及長期資本支持，未來獲利挑戰較大。
• 投資建議：看好中國AI長期發展者，可優先關注具資金、算力與應用場景優勢的大型平台企業，受惠程度可望高於多數AI新創
公司。

• 台灣9月外銷訂單再創新高：9月外銷訂單1,142.1億美元，連兩個月破千億美元，再創單月新高；年增率高達62.4%，連續
20個月呈雙位數正成長。經濟部表示，AI強勁需求超乎預期，預估今年第四季接單還會優於第三季。展望10月表現，外銷
訂單將持續亮麗，估落在1,125億至1,159億美元，有望連三個月破千億美元。
• 台股第四季有望先蹲後跳：10月初以來，台股在盤中觸及歷史高點49,968點後面臨一波修正，主要反映在新一輪的財報公
布前，市場觀望AI投資是否存在泡沫化風險。短期來看，台股在季線附近出現支撐，不過在11月初的美國期中選舉落幕前，
台股維持區間震盪的可能性較高；中期來看，美國期中選後往往因政治不確定性下降，歷史統計股市上漲機率高，加上台灣

年底集團作帳及選舉行情，正向看待年底台股表現。

### Page 1

Economic Indicators

2026 年 10 月30 日

1/3 
2026 年 7-9 月期 GDP 予測（1 次速報） 
～前期比年率＋0.4％と予想。成長率は鈍化も、景気回復の流れは崩れず～ 
経済調査部 シニアエグゼクティブエコノミスト 新家義貴 
（℡：050-5474-7490）

小幅ながら４四半期連続のプラス成長を予想、景気回復基調は維持 
11 月16 日に公表される2026 年７-９月期の実質ＧＤＰ成長率を前期比年率＋0.4％（前期比＋
0.1％）と予測する。４四半期連続のプラス成長になるだろう1。 
物価高による下押しや耐久財需要の反動に加え、天候不順の影響もあって個人消費が減少する
一方、設備投資が高い伸びになることが下支えになる見込みである。設備投資の伸びには特殊要
因も一部含まれているが、それを除いても堅調だったと考えられる。個人消費が弱含むなかでも
プラス成長を維持するとみられることは、企業部門の底堅さが景気を下支えしていることを示し
ている。過去３四半期に比較的高い成長が続いた後であることも踏まえれば、今回の減速をもっ
て景気の基調が弱まったと判断する必要はないだろう。家計部門の弱さは懸念材料だが、企業部
門の底堅さは維持されており、景気は引き続き緩やかな回復基調にあると評価できる。

輸入増による成長率押し下げと公的在庫のプラス寄与は表裏一体 
７-９月期のＧＤＰを評価する際には、原油輸入と公的在庫の動きをセットで捉える必要があ
る。４-６月期は、中東情勢の悪化によって原油輸入が激減したことが、ＧＤＰ統計上は輸入減を
通じて成長率を押し上げた。一方、輸入の減少による国内供給の不足を補うために国家備蓄原油
が放出され、公的在庫の取り崩しが進んだ。この結果、公的在庫変動は前期比寄与度▲0.5％Pt
（前期比年率寄与度▲2.1％Pt）と、成長率を大きく押し下げた。輸入減による成長率の押し上げ
は、公的在庫の減少と表裏一体の関係にあった。 
７-９月期は、これと逆の動きが生じるとみられる。政府・民間による代替調達の進展によって
原油輸入が回復するため、統計上は輸入増が成長率の押し下げ要因になる。一方、輸入の回復に
伴って国家備蓄原油の取り崩しは一服しており、公的在庫の減少幅は前期に比べて大きく縮小す
るとみられる。この結果、公的在庫変動は前期比寄与度＋0.4％Pt（前期比年率寄与度＋1.5％
Pt）と、成長率を大きく押し上げることが予想される。

1 10 月 30 日時点で入手可能な経済指標を元に作成している。国際収支や家計調査等、今後公表される経済指標の結果
を踏まえて予測値を修正する可能性がある。

### Page 2

2/3 
輸入増による成長率の押し下げと公的在庫変動による押し上げは、ともに原油の供給環境が改
善したことに伴う動きである。両者を個別に景気の強弱として評価することは適切ではなく、一
体として見るべきだろう。 
むしろ重要なのは、代替調達の進展によって国家備蓄への依存を減らしながら必要な原油を確
保できる状況になり、資源不足が生産活動を大きく制約するリスクが低下していることである。
ＧＤＰ統計上、輸入の増加は成長率の押し下げ要因となるものの、経済実態としては国内で利用
可能な資源の供給回復を意味している。この点は、７-９月期の日本経済を評価するうえで、むし
ろ前向きに捉えるべきだろう。

需要項目別の動向 
個人消費は前期比▲0.2％と小幅減少を予想する。８～９月に各地で相次いだ大雨や台風などの
天候不順が外出機会やサービス消費を抑制した可能性がある。また、４-６月期には自動車やエア
コンなどの耐久財販売が増加したが、７-９月期にはこうした一時的な押し上げ効果が剥落した。
加えて、物価高による節約志向が根強いなか、食料品や日用品などの非耐久財消費にも弱さがみ
られる。高い賃上げや底堅い雇用環境は下支え要因となっているものの、天候不順という一時的
な要因もあって個人消費は総じて力強さを欠く結果になったとみられる。 
設備投資は前期比＋2.0％と、高い伸びを予想する。４-６月期には特許権の海外譲渡に伴う研
究開発投資の減少という特殊要因があったが、７-９月期はその影響が剥落することが押し上げ要
因になった面はある。もっとも、こうした特殊要因を除いても設備投資は明確に増加したと考え
られ、底堅い動きとなっている。企業収益が高水準を維持するなか、人手不足に対応した省力化
投資やデジタル・ＡＩ関連投資への需要は根強く、企業の設備投資意欲は引き続き強い。設備投
資計画も高い伸びとなっており、設備投資は均してみれば緩やかな増加基調にあると判断してよ
いだろう。 
なお、特許権の海外への譲渡により、テクニカルに４-６月期はサービス輸出が上振れ、設備投
資が下振れたが、７-９月期はその剥落によりサービス輸出が下振れ、設備投資が上振れとなる見
込みである。両者は相殺し合うため、成長率全体でみれば概ねニュートラルとなる。 
輸出は前期比＋1.2％と４四半期連続の増加を予想する。４-６月期に大口要因によって研究開
発サービスの受取が急増した分が剥落することで、サービス輸出が減少する一方、財輸出が高い
伸びとなり、財貨・サービス輸出全体では増加を維持したとみられる。半導体製造装置などの資
本財輸出が好調だった。地域別では、ＥＵ向けが低調だった一方、ＡＳＥＡＮ、ＮＩＥｓ向けの
資本財輸出が伸びている。また、４-６月期に急減した中東向け輸出が持ち直したことも押し上げ
につながっている。 
輸入は前期比＋3.3％と大幅な増加が予想される。４-６月期には資源輸入の激減により輸入が
大幅に減少していたが、代替調達の進展により７-９月期は明確に持ち直した。この結果、外需寄
与度は前期比▲0.3％Pt（前期比年率▲1.3％Pt）になったと予想される。 
ただし、この外需寄与度のマイナスは、資源輸入の回復に伴う輸入の増加によって生じるもの

### Page 3

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
3/3 
である。その裏側では公的在庫変動が前期比＋0.4％ポイント、前期比年率では＋1.5％ポイント
の大幅プラス寄与になると見込まれる。両者は併せて評価する必要がある。 
民間在庫変動は前期比寄与度▲0.1％Ptと予想する。製品在庫はプラスが予想される一方、流通
在庫の下押しが大きくなるとみられる。 
住宅投資は前期比▲0.9％と、２四半期連続の減少を予想する。建設コストの上昇が下押し要因
となっており、住宅投資を取り巻く環境は引き続き厳しい。また、公共投資も同▲1.5％と減少を
予想するほか、政府消費についても、４-６月期の高い伸びの反動から前期比▲0.3％と減少を見
込む。

PMI 大幅走升、新屋銷售回穩，消費信心薄弱，美中關係暫現緩和訊號 
經濟 
⚫ S&P Global 公布，9 月美國採購經理人綜合指數(PMI Composite Output 
Index)初值由上月的 56.0 升至 58.4，創逾 5 年新高；服務業及製造業
PMI 分別升至 58.7 及 57.0。在內需強勁帶動下，新訂單與就業同步成
長，惟供應鏈瓶頸及能源成本攀升推升通膨壓力。 
⚫ 8 月美國新屋銷售年化率為 68.4 萬戶，月增 6.4%、年減 2.0%，顯示銷
售較前月回升但需求尚未完全恢復。中西部與南部為主要支撐，惟房價
年減、庫存仍維持 48 萬戶以上，供給相對充裕，後續銷售能否延續仍
待觀察。 
⚫ 9 月美國消費者信心指數降至 48.1，月減 7.0%、年減 12.7%，創近四個
月低點。當前狀況與預期指數同步下滑，反映高物價、燃料價格與貿易
不確定性持續壓抑信心；同時通膨預期升溫，後續消費動能仍待觀察。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 中國國家主席習近平於 9 月 23 日至 25 日訪美並與川普舉行峰會，雙方
同意延長貿易休兵至 2027 年 1 月 10 日，推動部分商品降稅、建立 AI
對話機制及持續磋商稀土供應議題，並延續高層溝通。台灣議題未列入
成果清單，關稅、科技競爭及供應鏈等核心分歧仍未化解，後續談判發
展仍待觀察。 
短評 
⚫ 美國 9 月 PMI 強勁擴張，顯示企業活動與就業動能仍具韌性，但投入
成本與售價上升也反映通膨壓力升溫。相較之下，房市需求尚未全面恢
復，消費信心則明顯轉弱，顯示內需前景仍有隱憂；美中延長貿易休兵
雖有助穩定市場，但核心分歧未解，後續不確定性仍高。

升息預期。

115.9.29 
一、 美國—PMI 大幅走升、新屋銷售回穩，消費信心薄弱，美中關係暫現緩和
訊號 
標普全球(S&P Global)公布，2026 年 9 月美國採購經理人綜合指數(PMI 
Composite Output Index)初值由上月的 56.0 升至 58.4，連續第四個月攀升，創 2021
年 7 月以來新高；其中服務業採購經理人指數(Services PMI)由 56.5 升至 58.7，創
59 個月高點，製造業採購經理人指數(Manufacturing PMI)由 53.9 升至 57.0，創
2022 年 5 月以來最高水準，顯示美國企業活動持續強勁擴張。受國內市場需求穩
健帶動，製造業與服務業新訂單同步加速成長，推升整體產出增速升至逾 5 年來
最快水準，企業亦積極擴增人力，就業成長速度創 2022 年 6 月以來新高。另一
方面，積壓訂單增加及供應鏈延遲加劇，反映產能利用率持續攀升；加以近期油
價上漲推升燃料及運輸成本，使企業投入成本增幅升至 2022 年 10 月以來最高，
帶動售價漲幅回升，顯示通膨壓力有所升溫。儘管如此，企業對未來一年營運前
景仍維持正向看法，反映市場對美國經濟韌性及需求持續擴張仍具信心。 
2026 年 8 月美國新屋銷售經季節性調整年化率為 68.4 萬戶，較上月增加 6.4%，
但較去年同期下降 2.0%，顯示新屋銷售較前月回升，惟仍低於去年同期水準。地
區別表現分歧，其中中西部及南部分別較上月增加 84.9%及 6.9%，東北部及西部
則分別減少 36.1%及 15.2%。價格方面，8 月新屋銷售中位價為 39.37 萬美元，較
上月小升 0.4%，但較去年同期下降 5.8%。供給方面，8 月底經季節性調整後待售
新屋數量為 48.3 萬戶，與上月持平，較去年同期減少 2.0%；按目前銷售速度估
算，供給月數為 8.5 個月，低於上月的 9.0 個月，與去年同期大致持平。整體而
言，8 月美國新屋銷售較前月回升，主要受中西部與南部銷售增加帶動，惟仍低
於去年同期，顯示需求動能尚未完全恢復。價格方面，中位價較去年同期下滑，
反映市場價格仍有調整壓力；同時待售新屋維持在 48 萬戶以上，供給月數仍達
8.5 個月，顯示整體供給相對充裕，後續仍須觀察銷售回升是否具持續性。 
密西根大學公布，2026 年 9 月美國消費者信心指數終值降至 48.1，低於上月
的 51.7，月減 7.0%、年減 12.7%，為近四個月低點，並較 2026 年 1 月下降約 15%，
顯示消費者信心持續轉弱。分項來看，當前經濟狀況指數由 51.9 降至 50.9，月減

1.9%、年減 15.7%；消費者預期指數則由 51.5 降至 46.3，月減 10.1%、年減 10.4%，
反映民眾對未來景氣與個人財務前景的看法明顯惡化。調查指出，消費者對高物
價的擔憂持續升高，當前與未來一年個人財務看法均較上月轉弱約一成；短期企
業展望也因燃料價格偏高與貿易爭端再度升溫而明顯下滑。政治立場方面，各陣
營受訪者普遍認為年初以來經濟前景轉弱，其中共和黨人信心較 1 月下降 20%，
民主黨人下降 13%。通膨預期方面，未來一年通膨預期由 8 月的 4.0%升至 4.6%，
為今年 6 月以來最高，且高於今年 2 月的 3.4%(美伊爆發衝突之前)及 2024 年各
月水準；長期通膨預期亦由 3.3%升至 3.4%，結束連續三個月持平，並高於 2024
年 2.8%至 3.2%的區間。整體而言，9 月美國消費信心再度降溫，顯示高物價、燃
料價格與貿易不確定性持續壓抑民眾信心，後續仍須觀察通膨預期升溫是否進一
步影響消費動能。 
中國國家主席習近平於 2026 年 9 月 23 日至 25 日訪問美國，並於 9 月 24 日
在華府白宮與美國總統川普舉行峰會。會後，美中雙方未發布聯合聲明，而是分
別公布成果清單，顯示雙方在穩定雙邊關係及維持高層溝通方面取得部分共識，
包括同意建構「基於尊重、公平、對等的建設性戰略穩定關係」、推動 300 億美
元非敏感商品對等降稅安排、建立人工智慧(AI)或「超級智慧」對話與風險溝通
機制，以及持續就稀土與關鍵礦產供應鏈議題進行磋商。此外，美方並表示中國
將於 2027 年及 2028 年分別進口至少 1,000 萬公噸美國煤炭。值得注意的是，台
灣議題並未出現在雙方公布的成果文件中；另一方面，中方則額外強調兩軍危機
溝通機制及合作搜尋失蹤美軍遺骸等事項。整體而言，本次川習會有助延續美中
對話與降低短期緊張情勢，惟雙方在關稅、科技競爭、供應鏈安全及其他戰略議
題上的結構性分歧仍未完全化解，後續談判進展及雙邊互動方向仍有待持續觀察。 
二、歐元區—9 月景氣增強，進一步推高升息預期 
 歐元區 9 月綜合採購經理人指數(PMI)增 1.1 點至 53.1，且為連續四個月上
升。其中服務業 PMI 增 1.4 點至 53，創近 10 個月高點，主要反映 AI 與國防投資
活動增加，帶動商業服務需求，抵銷能源價格上漲對消費購買力的負面影響。製
造業 PMI 則持平於 52.7，不過，德國萊茵河水位下降，沿岸較為發達的化工、製
藥業物流成本增加，因而暫時壓抑製造業上行空間。值得注意的是，由於國際原
油價格回升，綜合投入、產出物價指數皆上升至近 4 個月最高。故儘管 9 月經濟
表現優於預期，但通膨壓力也同步升溫，再加上先前歐洲央行的鷹派態度，使得

6

10 月升息一碼的可能性些微增加，亦強化未來升息預期，一般認為最終政策利率
(目前為 2.5%)將會落在 3%-3.25%。 
三、日本—9 月綜合 PMI 維持擴張，製造業表現仍具韌性 
 日本 9 月綜合 PMI 由 53.5 降至 52.5，製造業與服務業同步回落，經濟成長動
能較前兩個月的高檔有所降溫。製造業 PMI 下跌 0.8 點至 54.1，雖然產出、新訂
單及出口訂單增速放緩，但外部需求仍相當強勁，尤其 AI 相關需求可能持續帶
動出口表現，加上企業開始重建庫存、供應鏈瓶頸尚未完全解除，預料補庫存需
求將在未來數月繼續支撐生產成長。服務業 PMI 下降 0.9 點至 51.6，受到工資、
運輸及能源成本上升影響，需求動能略有降溫，但積壓訂單與出口業務有所改善。
另，投入成本與售價漲幅均較前期放緩，顯示通膨壓力正逐步緩解，但整體物價
水準仍維持高檔。此外，企業對未來景氣的信心持續改善，未來活動指數升至七
個月高點，反映企業普遍預期經濟擴張趨勢仍可望延續。 
四、中國—美中貿易延長休戰，惟後續協商成效仍待觀察 
1-8 月工業部門營業收入、利潤年增率分別為 6.6%、17.6%，其中營業收入年
增率較上期(1-7 月)略增 0.1 個百分點，但利潤年增率下降 1.9 個百分點。受惠於
金屬、塑化原物料上漲，加上 AI 需求擴張，以及政府推動高科技產業發展等因
素，營收成長貢獻度較高者包含有色金屬加工(營收、利潤年增 31.1%、71.8%)、
計算機、通信和其他電子設備製造業(營收、利潤年增 20.1%、109.9%)、化學原料
及製品(營收、利潤年增 9.1%、51%)。此外，電氣機械及器材業、汽車業營收年
增率雖分別為 11.5%、2.9%，但獲利分別年減 5.2%、16%。非金屬礦物製品業營
收、獲利下滑 9.5、46.7%，黑色金屬加工業營收、獲利分別年減 1.1%、62.4%，
反映產業持續進行減產所致。整體而言，部分產業受惠於短期原物料價格波動，
以及受到政策推動的高科技產業表現較佳，惟部分產業因陷入產能調整週期，或
政府減少支持等因素而表現較弱，產業分化明顯。 
展望未來，儘管美中雙方互相降低等值 300 億美元產品關稅，惟考量雙方每
年貿易總額逾 4,000 億美元，且降稅產品並非半導體、先進製程設備、電動車、
軍民兩用技術等關鍵領域，因此實質效益有限。另外，雖然美中雙方同意延長貿
易休戰，但休戰期只延長兩個月，反映美國仍保留談判槓桿，持續給予中國稀土
供應履約的壓力。後續將關注美中能否在休戰期間就開放市場、科技競爭、供應

用相關內容。

7

鏈安全等議題取得進一步共識，若談判不如預期，美國仍可能重新祭出新的反制
措施，而中國亦可能運用稀土作為談判籌碼，經貿關係不確定性仍是較高。此外，
預計 10 月 8 日舉行歐中貿易會談，若談判未能取得突破，歐盟對中國的貿易限
制恐進一步升級，對中國出口形成額外壓力。 
五、印度及東南亞—亞銀上修亞洲及太平洋經濟體 2026 年 GDP 預估至 4.7% 
亞 洲 開 發 銀 行 (ADB) 最 新 經 濟 展 望 (Asian Development Outlook, ADO 
September 2026)上修 2026 年亞洲及太平洋地區經濟體 2026 年經濟成長率(GDP)
至 4.7%(7 月預估 4.5%)、2027 年為 4.5%。ADB 認為，亞洲開發中經濟體雖面臨
中東衝突延長、俄烏戰爭再升溫、能源價格高檔震盪及強烈聖嬰現象等逆風，但
在政府刺激政策、民間投資及 AI 帶動科技出口支撐下，區域經濟仍展現韌性。
通膨方面，2026 年通膨率下修至 3.9%，惟能源與糧食價格壓力仍高，2027 年通
膨預測上修至 3.3%。 
區域經濟表現方面，南亞成長最強勁，2026 年 GDP 成長率預估上修至 6.4%，
主要受印度內需與投資帶動；東亞則受中國內需疲弱影響，成長維持平穩；東協
國家(ASEAN)上調至 4.7%，其中越南、馬來西亞及泰國受惠於科技與製造業投資
而獲上修。中亞及西亞、太平洋地區則面臨能源及食品價格上漲壓力，成長預測
略為下修至 3.7%、3.0%。(詳下表) 
ADB 認為未來最大風險包括地緣政治衝突升級、聖嬰現象造成糧食供應衝
擊，以及 AI 相關資產可能出現價格重估。整體而言，AI 驅動的科技投資仍是亞
洲經濟體最重要的成長動能來源，而能源、糧食及氣候風險將成為影響各國通膨
與信用風險的重要變數。

五、台灣—AI 浪潮推升接單動能，外銷訂單再創新高 
8 月外銷訂單達 1,029.6 億美元，首度突破千億美元大關，其中受惠於 ASIC
晶片需求帶動，資通產品接單翻倍、電子產品接單亦大增 83.9%，惟光學器材因
面板需求維持低檔，年減 5%。非科技產品方面，基本金屬年增 26.8%，係因銅箔
及銅箔基板受惠 AI 相關供應鏈需求及銅價續漲，加以鍍鋅鋼需求回溫；機械年
增 28.3%，係因半導體業者增加資本支出，帶動半導體設備、自動化設備需求延
續；化學品年增 15.4%，主因藥品接單成長，加上產業用化學品需求持續增加；
塑橡膠製品年增 6.7%，主係塑膠原料受原料上漲而推升產品售價，加上部分塑膠
製品訂單回溫。展望未來，經濟部統計處預估 2026 年外銷訂單有望突破 1 兆美
元，在 AI 相關需求持續擴張帶動下，科技產品表現可望維持強勁，而傳統產業
中下游產品亦可望受惠於相關供應鏈需求，惟上游原物料價格競爭激烈，後續仍
須關注第四季消費旺季是否有相關備貨需求。

⚫ 長期佈局機器人與 CPO 商機 
機器人業務佔 26Q3 營收達 12%，其中 EFEM 隨半導體需
求熱絡穩健成長，並已超前部署對應 FOPLP 方片規格之移載
系統。公司充分發揮垂直整合優勢，自製減速機、螺桿、軸承，
並整合關係企業大銀微系統之伺服馬達與驅動器。目前已獲美
系人形機器人廠商小批量線性滑軌訂單，美系 AI 物流機器人
放量時程則取決於客戶端實測進度。CPO 方面，大銀微已開
發出奈米級定位平台，並整合上銀微小型螺桿與線軌，共同搶
攻次世代半導體設備商機。

⚫ 結論與建議: 
26Q3 受惠終端需求強勁，營收達 81.10 億元，QoQ 8.01%，
YoY 35.27%；受惠稼動率提升與價格策略帶動毛利走高，單
季 EPS 達 3.48 元。10 月營收延續強勁動能，達 28.50 億元，
MoM 4.79%，YoY 35.66%，累計前 10 月營收為 248.48 億元，
YoY 25.11%。目前整體市場供不應求，訂單能見度已直達
2027 年中。    
展望後市，隨公司下半年增聘逾 300 名員工並持續加班生產，
26 年底產出量將較年初成長 25%至 30%，預期 26Q4 將打破
歐美長假與盤點淡季效應，營運有望優於 26Q3。研究部預估
26Q4 營收為 85.10 億元，QoQ 4.94%，YoY 30.86%；毛利
率估為 35.11%，營業利益 12.53 億元，QoQ -13.32%，YoY 
297.33%；單季 EPS 為 2.95 元。    
綜合考量下游客戶需求持續暢旺、公司優先承接高毛利訂單使
獲利結構性優化，並已長期佈局機器人與 CPO 商機，研究部
預估 26 與 27 年營收為 305.08 億元與 344.40 億元，YoY 
25.74%與 12.89%；毛利率為 34.54%與 35.11%；營業利益
為 43.63 億元與 58.60 億元，YoY 165.52%與 34.30%；稅後
獲利為 36.93 億元與 48.07 億元，YoY 142.05%與 30.15%；
預估 26 與 27 年 EPS 分別為 10.44 元與 13.59 元。    
評價方面，上銀過去五年本益比主要集中於 20 倍至 40 倍區
間。考量公司整體營收與獲利將呈現雙位數之成長上升趨勢，
且自動化與傳動產業結構持續復甦上行，研究部以 2027 年預
估 EPS 13.59 元作為評價基礎，並給予歷史區間中緣偏上之
33 倍 PER，目標價設定為 450 元，給予買進之投資建議。

2344 華邦電(Winbond)

報告日期：2026/11/06 研 究 員：王蕎程 (Joe Wang) 
壹、訪 談／法 說 內 容 
重點摘要 
 3Q26 稅後 EPS 7.58 元，預估 4Q26 為 8.06 元。 受惠記憶體
報價上漲與產品組合改善，第三季毛利率達 70.57%，預估第
四季營收 839.78 億元，QoQ +2.0%，毛利率 70.77%。 
 擴產與 16 奈米轉進帶動 2027 年位元出貨成長。 高雄廠
Module A 新增產能及台中廠 Flash 擴產陸續貢獻，搭配
DDR4、LPDDR4 及高容量 NOR Flash 需求，支持營運成長。 
 預估 2026／2027 年稅後 EPS 23.29／37.45 元。 AI 伺服器與
企業級 SSD 需求為主要動能，CUBE 及矽電容拓展新應用。

一、3Q26 稅後 EPS 7.58 元，預估 4Q26 營收持續季增 
華邦電為利基型記憶體廠，產品涵蓋客製化記憶體、NOR Flash 及
SLC NAND。3Q26 營收 823.31 億元，QoQ +37.6%、YoY +278.2%；
營業毛利 581.01 億元，毛利率 70.57%；營業利益 437.84 億元，營
業利益率 53.18%；稅後純益 354.22 億元， EPS 7.58 元。營收結構
以客製化記憶體占 58%、Flash 占 32%、邏輯 IC 占 9%。其中，客
製化記憶體平均售價季增近 60%，Flash 平均售價季增約 30%，為
第三季成長主因。展望第四季，公司表示 AI 需求維持強勁，部分
PC 與消費性客戶調整拉貨，整體營運仍將成長，惟增幅低於前幾
季。考量合約價格續升及產品組合改善，預估 4Q26 營收 839.78 億
元，QoQ +2.0%；毛利率 70.77%，營業利益 448.28 億元，稅後 EPS 
8.06 元。

二、高雄廠擴產，支應 DDR4 及 LPDDR4 長期需求 
公司持續供應 DDR3、DDR4 及 LPDDR4，受惠主要記憶體原廠調
整產品配置，以及車用、工業客戶產品生命週期長、替換供應來
源不易，長期供貨需求具支撐。高雄廠 Module A 月產能預計由約
1.5 萬片提高至 2.4 萬至 2.5 萬片，新增產能於 2027 年陸續貢獻，
並提高 16 奈米製程比重，同時增加晶圓投入與單片晶圓位元產
出。法人看好擴產搭配製程轉進可帶動 2027 年 DRAM 位元出貨顯
著成長，中信預估增幅超過 100%。本報告採新增產能與製程效益
逐步反映的假設，預估 2027 年營收逐季成長。長期方面，董事會
通過 731 億元資本支出，用於高雄廠 Module B 土建、機電及長交
期設備，規劃月產能約 5 萬至 6 萬片，預計 2029 年下半年開始小
量出貨，並以長期供貨協議支應策略客戶需求。

三、AI 帶動 CUBE 與矽電容拓展新應用 
AI 伺服器機櫃內 GPU、BMC、SmartNIC 及交換器等元件均需 Flash
儲存韌體與設定資料，隨運算及管理架構複雜化，帶動使用顆數
與容量提升。公司預估供應 AI 生態系的 Flash 位元出貨量於 2026
年成長 112%，2027 年再增 67%，其中高容量 NOR Flash 為主要動
能；企業級 SSD 亦增加 DRAM 需求，擴大公司在伺服器供應鏈的
參與。法人看好台中廠擴產與高容量產品組合改善。新產品方面，
CUBE 主要布局 AI 加速器與推論應用，預計 2027 年下半年增加出
貨；矽電容已小量出貨，既有產能多獲客戶預訂，並透過代工模
式切入 AI 晶片、先進封裝及高速光模組應用，逐步增加產品附加
價值與長期營收來源。 
四. 投資建議：買進，目標價 180 元 
華邦電為全球最大 NOR Flash 廠亦是利基型 DRAM 廠，AI 帶動各
式記憶體需求增加，產能仍供不應求下，獲利將逐季成長。公司
針對邊緣 AI 發展之 CUBE 解決方案將於 2027 年開始貢獻，矽電容
亦為新的業務機會，中長期而言 AI、HPC、IoT、自駕車等新產品
新應用將帶動更多記憶體需求。考量到 1) AI 帶動之記憶體供不應
求將延續至 2028 年、2)華邦電今明年營收獲利創高、3)CUBE、矽
電容具成長潛力，參考 2027 年 EPS 給予 5 倍本益比，目標價 180
元，建議買進。

貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況

一.公司簡介 
華邦電子於 1987 年九月由華新麗華集團轉投資成立，為自有品牌
IC 設計及製造廠商，並為全世界第五大 DRAM 供應商，且為國內
少數擁有晶圓廠之積體電路設計公司，同時也是全球利基型記憶
體的主要供應商。2008 年公司將邏輯 IC 事業分割成立新唐科技，
新唐產品線以消費性電子 IC、電腦 IC 兩大產品線為主，並同時擁
有一座 6 吋晶圓廠。華邦電在 NOR Flash 為全球最大供應商，產
品上從中低容量市場拓展至中高容量市場。NOR Flash 主要同業為
旺宏、兆易創新、Infineon。

二.2026 年營運概況及展望

1. 2026 下半年記憶體維持上漲格局，預估 2026 年 EPS 23.29 元 
展望 2026 下半年，預估記憶體求維持強勁下，價格持續上漲，唯
因價格基期已高，加上傳統消費性產品追價力道有限，漲幅不若
上半年，不過預估今年隨著價格上漲，加上製程轉換帶動位元出

或個股之相關條件改變，投資人需自行考量投資之實際狀況與風險，本公司恕不負擔任何法律責任及做任何保

貨量增加，及新產能貢獻，營收逐季成長，毛利率持續提升，預
估華邦電 2026 年營收 2,644.05 億元(YoY+195.7%)，毛利率 67.17%，
稅後淨利 1,047.92 億元(YoY+2,545.0%)，EPS 23.29 元。

3. 2027 年記憶體更加緊缺，預估 EPS 37.45 元 
展望 2027 年，AI 帶動之記憶體缺貨狀況延續，加上全球新增產能
有限，緊缺情況更加嚴峻，而儘管終端電子產品需求不一，不過
未來 AI 落地後每單位產品之記憶體搭載量持續成長，使得記憶體
位元出貨提升，2027 年公司營運仍正向。DDR4 在全球三大廠逐步
退出下價格自 2Q25 起漲，且因終端電子產品仍有一定需求，價格
將維持高檔，NOR Flash 及 SLC NAND 亦因供給端增產有限，價格
逐步回升，其中 SLC Nand 因大廠退出，華邦將成為全球最大供應
商，而應用於 AI Server 的高容量 NOR Flash 持續供不應求，預期
將推升價格上行至 2027 年。華邦電製程技術方面，20 奈米 DRAM
產品仍為主要出貨主力，應用於 DDR3、DDR4、LPDDR 產品，16
奈米製程已開發完成，2027 年將有一定比例轉換至 16 奈米；NOR 
Flash 營收穩定成長，NAND Flash 24 奈米及 32 奈米 SLC Nand 今年
2Q 貢獻顯著提升。因應記憶體需求強勁，公司 Flash 與 DRAM 擴
產計畫進行中，華邦電 2025 年資本支出為台幣 55 億元，2026 年
將提升至 395 億元，其中生產設備資本支出約占 95%，大部分用
於高雄產能及製程提升，少部分用於台中廠製程提升，高雄廠產
能將由現在 15k 擴充至年底的 24k，屆時位元出貨量可望翻倍，同
時啟動高雄 Module B 投資計劃，規劃月產能可達 50k~60k，將分
階段擴充，預估 2027 年 1 月動工，2029 年 1 月裝機，2029 年底量
產。2027 年預估 DRAM、NOR Flash 及 SLC NAND 價格持續上漲帶
動營收獲利成長，預估 2027 年營收 3,833.59 億元(YoY+45.0%)，毛
利率 72.29%，稅後淨利 1,685.15 億元(YoY+60.8%)，EPS 37.45 元。

4. CUBE 及矽電容為新成長動能 
AI 時代來臨，華邦電長期專注於利基型 DRAM，針對 AI 將主攻邊
緣運算市場，邊緣運算之記憶體容量不需像 AI Server 那麼高，為
華邦電可著墨之區塊，因此華邦電提供 CUBE(Customized Ultra 
Bandwidth Element 客製化超高頻寬記憶體元件)解決方案，CUBE
具備超低功耗和超高性能、小尺寸、高成本效益，從而大幅提升
系統能力、回應時間和能源效率，以滿足邊緣運算平臺上快速增
長的 AI 應用需求。CUBE 優點在於 1)SoC 推疊在上面，散熱效果
較好、2) 可在 CUBE Die 上做穿孔(TSV)，降低成本、3) 3D 推疊
適合較小的終端裝置。目前華邦電與多家客戶開發 CUBE 方案，
預估 2026 年底開始貢獻，2027 年放量。另外因應 AI 晶片功耗劇
增、瞬間電流波動，以及先進封裝的空間限制，矽電容具有極低
的等效串聯電感與寄生電感，能在極短時間內補償電流，維持電

源完整性，避免電壓驟降，也彌補傳統 MLCC 的不足，華邦電因
客戶需求提供矽電容 foundry 代工，目前產能已滿載，未來 CUBE
及矽電容均為新成長動能。

叁、業 績 及 獲 利 預 估

一. 3Q26 毛利率升至 70.57%，EPS 7.58 元 
華邦電 3Q26 營收 823.31 億元(QoQ+37.6%、YoY+278.2%)，其中記
憶體事業營收 742.84 億元(QoQ+43.7%)。受惠客製化記憶體平均售
價季增近 60%、Flash 平均售價季增約 30%，合併毛利率提升至
70.57%，營業利益 437.84 億元，歸屬母公司稅後淨利 354.22 億元，
EPS 7.58 元。展望第四季，記憶體合約價格持續上升，惟部分 PC
與消費性客戶調整拉貨，預估營收 839.78 億元(QoQ+2.0%)，毛利
率 70.77%，EPS 8.06 元。

二. 2026 年記憶體維持上漲格局，預估 EPS 23.29 元 
展望 2026 年下半年，AI 伺服器需求強勁，帶動 DDR4、LPDDR4
及高容量 Flash 價格持續上漲，惟第四季部分 PC 與消費性客戶調
整拉貨，營運成長幅度較第三季收斂。預估華邦電 2026 年營收
2,644.05 億元(YoY+195.7%)，毛利率 67.17%，稅後淨利 1,047.92 億
元(YoY+2,545.0%)，EPS 23.29 元。

三. 2027 年擴產與製程轉進帶動成長，預估 EPS 37.45 元 
展望 2027 年，AI 伺服器與企業級 SSD 需求持續成長，DDR4、
LPDDR4 及高容量 NOR Flash 供需維持緊俏，加上高雄廠新增產能
與 16 奈米製程轉進，帶動位元出貨增加，CUBE 及矽電容亦逐步
貢獻營收。預估 2027 年營收 3,833.59 億元(YoY+45.0%)，毛利率
72.29%，稅後淨利 1,685.15 億元(YoY+60.8%)，EPS 37.45 元。

### Page 1

M
Update
Broader recovery supports record earnings. 3Q revenue was NT$597.3bn (+1% Q/
Q, +82% Y/Y), with gross margin of 3.6% (+0.2ppt Q/Q, -0.1ppt Y/Y, 0.3ppt above
MSe), and net profit of NT$10.3bn (+6% Q/Q, +170% Y/Y, 42% above MSe). Data-
center revenue fell 5% Q/Q on shipment timing, while industrial, automotive and
Future Electronics revenue increased sequentially. The richer mix lifted gross margin
19bp Q/Q to 3.60%; operating margin rose 18bp to 2.38%, with operating expenses

increasing only 2% Q/Q. Net financing costs rose 22% Q/Q to NT$1.94bn.
AI drives a stronger 4Q. Management guides 4Q revenue of NT$730-770bn, with
the NT$750bn midpoint up 26% Q/Q, as data-center and communications growth
outweigh seasonal declines across consumer applications, industrial and automotive.
The guided gross-margin midpoint falls 36bp Q/Q to 3.24% on mix, but operating
profit rises 19% Q/Q to NT$16.96bn as expenses grow only 1%. 4Q EPS guidance is

NT$8.07–9.05, with the NT$8.56 midpoint up 7% Q/Q. Financing costs of NT$2.6–
2.8bn and the larger weighted-average share base temper the translation from
operating-profit growth into EPS.
Growth broadens, but cash conversion matters. Future Electronics grew over 40%
Y/Y in 3Q and has started shipping data-center products. Management remains
positive on 2027 AI demand, without providing a full-year earnings forecast.
Inventory days increased Q/Q from 47 to 66  and net debt from NT$27.6bn to NT

$46.4bn, although longer payables kept the cash-conversion cycle near 19 days
versus 18 in 2Q. We see further earnings potential from AI scale and Future’s
recovery, with funding requirements and inventory conversion the key execution
risks.
What’s Changed

Valuation methodology
We cut our price target by 15% to  NT$296: We update our residual income model to
reflect earnings estimate revisions. We increase our equity risk premium assumption from
11.8% to 12.5% to reflect segment volatilities, and reduce our intermediate growth rate
assumption from 10% to 8% to reflect a more conservative medium-term growth forecast.
We keep other key RI assumptions unchanged: 2.0% risk-free rate, 0.9 beta, cash dividend

payout ratio of 85%, and a long-term growth rate of 3.0%. Bull case PT is cut from NT
$406 to NT$344.5, implying 10.6x 2027 P/E. Bear case PT is cut from NT$230 to NT$160,
implying 4.9x 2027 P/E. The risk reward is now skewed more towards the bear case due to
potential downstream demand disruption.

Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

Lite-On Technology  | Asia Pacific
October  Sales +6% MoM/+32%
YoY
In this report, we focus on Lite-On's monthly sales, which we
believe could be a catalyst for its share price.
Details:
• October sales came in at NT$20,071mn (+6% MoM/+32% YoY), driven by the
server PSU/BBU and opto semiconductors (couplers and indicators for
industrial automation, new energy and AI applications).
• QTD revenue reached 33% of both MSe at NT$60,395mn (+5% QoQ/+36%

YoY), and consensus at NT$60,353mn (+5% QoQ/+36% YoY).
• By segment, Cloud & AIoT/Optoelectronics/IT&CE accounted for
66%/13%/21% of the monthly sales, respectively, implying growth of +19%/-
1%/-17% MoM and  +71%/+7%/-16% YoY.
Our view:
• We think its 4Q26 revenue is tracking slightly ahead of market expectations.
• We believe Lite-On will grow along with the AI capex cycle driven by its
power-related offerings, though execution and progress with new customers

remain key to watch.
• Stay EW.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Lite-On Technology (2301.TW)
Base case: P/E multiple of 15x based on average 2027-28 EPS estimates. We believe our target
multiple is justified by the 50% earnings CAGR we estimate for 2025-28, implying a PEG of
0.3, vs. the stock's 16x P/E and 2% earnings CAGR during 2022-25, which implied a PEG of 7.2.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger optical business growth
n Stronger data center/server demand

n PC/NB demand growth
n Downtrend in raw material prices
Risks to Downside
n Macro demand weakness
n Weaker data center/server demand
n PC/NB demand slowdown
n Uptrend in raw material prices

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

### Page 2

Earnings revision 
We revise down our 2026 earnings estimates by 8% to factor in 3Q26 results (we revise 
up our 2026E revenue estimates by 1%). We revise down 2027/28E earnings estimates 
by 9%/8% to reflect product mix with higher contribution from the server business (we 
revise up our 2027/28E revenue estimates by 6/6%). We now expect the GM expansion 
to be slower due to the higher server business contribution that is margin dilutive, thus 
revise down GPM by 0.2/0.2/0.2ppt in 2026/27/28E.

Valuation:  
We reiterate our Neutral rating on Inventec with a new 12m TP of NT$56 (from NT$61), 
based on 14.2x 2027E P/E (unchanged), which is the company’s average P/E for the past 
10 years, and factoring in our earnings revision.

### Page 3

Investment thesis and Valuation & Risks

Inventec is an ODM company focusing on the design and manufacturing of PCs, servers, 
and consumer electronics. While we like Inventec’s solid position in the AI server ODM 
market, we are Neutral rated as we believe valuation already reflects near-term 
positives. We model Inventec earnings growth in 2026-28E to be driven by the 
commercial PC demand recovery and AI server ramp-up. We model AI servers to 
contribute more in 2026-28E. We see P/E valuation as fair at the current level compared 
to its peers and already reflects the solid growth in 2026-28E. Catalysts for further 
upside/downside would be its customers’ demand on AI servers and general server 
demand outlook.

Valuation methodology: Our 12m TP of NT$56 is based on 14.2x 2027E P/E, which is 
the company’s average P/E for the past 10 years. 
Key upside/downside risks: (1) stronger-/weaker-than-expected PC market recovery; 
(2) faster-/slower-than-expected AI server ramp-up; and (3) 
stronger-/weaker-than-expected demand for general servers.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.

RA 배현서 
hyeonseo.bae@meritz.co.kr 배당은 지켜질 것  
3Q26 지배주주 순이익, 컨센서스 -38% 하회 
매출 31.6조원 (+10% YoY)으로 컨센서스에 부합했다. 출고 판매 (+10% YoY), 소
매 판매 (+8% YoY) 모두 높은 성장을 기록하며, 외형 확대에 기여했다. 
영업이익 1.82조원 (+24% YoY)으로 컨센서스를 -25% 하회했다. 전년 동기 대미 
관세 25% 부과에 따른 기저효과 +5,330억원 (2025년 11월부터 15%로 하향)이 
존재했고 기말 환율 (원/달러 -12% QoQ 강세) 변동에 따른 판매보증 충당부채 환
입 +6,700억원을 인식했음에도 불구하고, 신규 출시 저가 BEV 모델들의 판매 확대
에 따른 전사 평균 판매가격 하락  할인 경쟁 심화  원재료 및 운송비 상승으로 부
진한 실적을 기록했다. 
지배주주 순이익 1.29조원 (-10% YoY)으로 컨센서스를 -38% 하회했다. 영업 실적 
부진과 함께 원화 강세에 의한 외화자산 평가손실 및 관계사 실적 악화에 따른 지분
법 이익 감소 (-36% YoY)가 더해진 결과다.

6,000원 이상 배당 유지 전망 
4Q26 이후 실적 방향도 먹구름이 가득하다. 원/달러 환율의 YoY 강세 반영이 시작
된다. 미국 시장 내 경쟁력 확보를 위해 가장 중요한 원/엔 환율의 방향성 또한 비우
호적 흐름이 지속 중이다. 내수 시장을 포함해 전세계 주요 시장 내 중국산 차량과의 
경쟁 강도도 높아지고 있다. 원유 가격 뿐만 아니라 알루미늄, 구리 등 핵심 원재료 
가격의 상승 반영도 이어지고 있다. 
2026년 연간 지배주주 순이익 6.9조원 (-9% YoY)을 전망한다. 주주환원 총액은 축
소될 것이다. 다만 자사주 매입을 줄이더라도 배당은 전년 수준을 지킬 것으로 기대
한다. 참고로 2025년 실적에 기반한 주주환원은 자사주 매입 0.9조원, 배당 2.6조원 
(주당 6,800원)이었으며, 주주환원율은 48%였다. 현대차그룹은 Boston Dynamics
에 대한 증자를 이어가고 있다. 정의선 회장도 마찬가지다. 그리고 기아의 배당은 현
대차  현대글로비스의 배당과 함께 증자 재원으로 활용되고 있다. 6,000원 이상의 
배당 유지를 기대하며, 6% 이상 시가 배당 수익률 제시를 통해 주가 하방 경직성 확
보가 가능하다고 판단한다. 기업가치 상승 재개를 위해서는 기존 차량 제조  판매 사
업을 넘어 피지컬 데이터 디바이스 제공 사업 진출 가시성 확보가 필요하다. 
 Meritz Research  2026. 11. 2

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준) 
적정주가 (12개월) 150,000원 
현재주가 (10.30) 95,600원 
상승여력 56.9%

### Page 1

(005930) 
삼성전자 
2026.09.29

반도체 손인준 
6154 / sij@eugenefn.com

긴 호흡으로 
 투자의견 STRONG BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 560,000 원(유지) 
 현재주가 270,000 원(9/28)

 삼성전자의 3Q26 영업이익이 103.6조원(+16%qoq)을 기록할 것으로 전
망한다. 메모리 가격 등 전반적인 반도체 업황에 대한 기존 가정은 대체로 
유지했으나, 최근 원화 강세에 따라 원/달러 환율 가정을 1,440원에서 
1,400원으로 낮추고 MX 사업부의 적자 확대를 반영해 실적 추정치를 하
향 조정했다. 또한 2Q26 재고가 3Q26 매출로 인식되는 과정에서 발생하
는 추가 성과급 충당금 부담도 반영했다.  
 DS 부문의 3Q26 영업이익은 104.0조원(+17%qoq)을 기록할 것으로 전
망한다. 서버 DRAM과 eSSD 중심의 가격 인상이 이어지며, DRAM과 
NAND의 Blended ASP는 각각 +17%, +18%QoQ 상승할 것으로 추정한
다. 1cnm 수율 개선과 HBM4의 빠른 램프업도 실적 개선에 기여할 전망이
다. Foundry/LSI는 HBM4 베이스다이 생산 확대, Groq 3 LPU 양산 및 선
단 공정 가격 인상에 힘입어 하반기 매출 성장이 예상된다. 다만 성과급 충
당금과 감가상각비 부담으로 영업적자 축소 폭은 제한적일 것으로 보인다. 
 DX 부문의 3Q26 영업이익은 -1.8조원(적자지속qoq)을 기록할 것으로 추
정한다. 삼성전자는 메모리 가격 급등에도 제품 가격 인상을 제한하며 시장 
점유율 확대에 주력하고 있다. 이러한 가격 정책과 갤럭시 Z 폴드8 등 신제
품 흥행에 힘입어 판매량은 당초 예상치를 상회할 것으로 보인다. 다만 원
가 상승분의 전가가 제한되면서 영업적자 폭은 크게 확대될 전망이다.  
 삼성전자의 4Q26 영업이익은 114.9조원(+11%qoq)을 기록할 것으로 전
망한다. DRAM과 NAND의 Blended ASP는 각각 +11%, +12%QoQ 상승
하며 견조한 흐름을 이어갈 것으로 보인다. 환율 가정 조정에 따라 2026년
과 2027년 영업이익 추정치를 각각 365.2조원(+735%yoy), 595.8조원
(+63%YoY)으로 낮췄다. 4Q26~1Q27 범용 DRAM·NAND 및 2027년 
HBM 가격 협상 환경은 공급업체에 유리하게 전개되고 있어, 향후 가격 가
정의 추가 상향 가능성이 높다고 판단한다.  
 극심한 DRAM 공급 부족으로 인한 HBM 적층 단수 하향 조정(base die 수
요 증가), Memory Wall에 대응하기 위한 3D Stacked DRAM·zHBM 도입 
논의 등은 파운드리의 기술적 가치를 부각시키는 요인이다. 파운드리의 수
익성 회복 속도는 완만할 전망이지만, 메모리와 파운드리를 연계한 턴키 비
즈니스의 시너지는 AI 반도체 시장 내 경쟁력을 높이고, 메모리 시장 점유
율 확대에도 기여할 것으로 보인다. 적극적인 메모리 가격 인상 정책으로 
업계 최고 수준의 Blended ASP도 장기간 유지할 것으로 예상한다. 
 이제 메모리 업종 주가는 업황의 지속 가능성을 확인하고 꾸준한 주주환원
을 반영하며, 중장기적으로 점진적인 상승세를 이어갈 것으로 판단한다. 삼
성전자는 메모리·파운드리를 아우르는 턴키 역량과 가격 정책에 기반한 실
적 차별화를 통해 피어 그룹 내 경쟁력을 높여갈 것으로 보인다.

Compliance Notice 
당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3 개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에도 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

3Q26Pre: 환율 하락에도 성장 흐름 유지 
매출액 1조 3,477억원(+7.2%YoY, 이하 YoY 생략), 영업이익 5,957억원(-6.0%, OPM 
44.2%)로 컨센서스에 부합할 것으로 전망. 3분기 원달러 평균 환율은 1,423원으로 전분기 
평균 환율 대비 약 5.2% 하락에도 불구하고 성장 흐름 지속. 
2분기 노조 파업으로 인한 영향은 3분기 생산을 진행했으며 4분기 매출에 반영될 예정으로 
연간 실적에 미치는 영향은 제한적일 것으로 예상. GSK로부터 인수한 록빌 공장도 안정적
으로 생산이 이루어지고 있으며 CAPA 여유분은 추가 수주를 진행 중으로 28~29년 이후 
완전 가동 전망. 
4Q, 6공장 건설과 폴리펩타이드 인수 
유상 증자 관련 투자 설명서 상 투자 계획에 따르면 6공장 투자는 26년부터 29년까지 진행 
될 예정으로 29년 가동 목표. 6공장은 5공장과 동일한 18만 리터 규모로 건설되며, 연내 투
자 집행을 시작함에 따라 건설 발표도 임박한 것으로 추정. 동사는 일정 수준 이상 기존 공
장 수주를 확보한 후 후속 공장 건설을 진행해오고 있어 non-binding 수주 계약의 본계약 
전환도 순차적으로 이루어질 것으로 전망. 
폴리펩타이드 인수도 현재 공개 매수를 진행 중에 있으며 연내 마무리될 것으로 예상. 
1H26 폴리펩타이드 매출은 2.37억 유로(+41.6%)로 GLP-1 agonist 등 펩타이드 의약품 
수요 증가에 따라 높은 성장 지속. 
투자 의견 Buy, 목표 주가 200만원으로 하향 
매수 의견을 유지하며 목표 주가는 기존 210만원에서 200만원으로 하향. 목표 주가 하향은 
유상 증자에 따른 EPS 희석에 기인. 글로벌 항체 CDMO 수요가 여전히 견조하며, 폴리펩
타이드 인수로 펩타이드로의 확장에 따른 추가적인 성장 기대. 주주 대상 유상 증자 영향과 
파업 우려 등이 있으나 노사 합의 시 주가 반등 전망.

Appendix

Document 341: FX Outlook 2027_ Seven key calls

Source PDF: all report pdf/FX Outlook 2027_ Seven key calls.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 12
Status: ok

### Page 1

We expect 2027 to be a low-volatility year in FX, with central
banks and rate diﬀerentials ﬁrmly in the driving seat. We see a
dollar decline resuming from 2Q27, while diverging monetary,
growth and external dynamics create opportunities across
regions

Chris Turner, Francesco Pesole, Frantisek Taborsky and Deepali
Bhargava
Winners and losers in FX next year

### Page 2

Source: ING, Reﬁnitiv
Not a breakout year for volatility
Our most important FX call for 2027 is the market environment. Our
macro-driven call is that lower oil prices and only a modest
recalibration in global monetary policy mean that FX volatility can
stay low. This is not 2022/2023, when major central banks
belatedly delivered 500bp tightening cycles to deal with double-
digit inﬂation. If 2026 has shown us anything, it is that FX markets
can prove quite resilient in this high-growth, high-inﬂation global

economy. And markets have proved quite resilient to geopolitical
risk as well.
For the majority of the last ﬁve years, the market has
overestimated FX volatility. There may be some structural factors at
play here, such as huge growth in ﬁnancial assets bringing in more
liquidity to the FX market and the greater use of FX pricing algos on
the buy-side, limiting the market fallout when trading conditions
thin.
Yet market volatility has not vanished. There are plenty of risks out

there, which we highlight in a dedicated section below. The same
applies for market liquidity. If the shock is big enough – such as
Liberation Day tariﬀs in 2025 or the start of the Iran war this year –
market depth can fall away quickly. The prospect of fat tails, low-
probability, high-impact event risks, is certainly a threat to global
FX markets. But the bar for these risks to raise volatility is a high
one.

### Page 3

Volatility has mostly undershot expectations
in 2026
Source: ING, Reﬁnitiv
Look no further than central banks in
G10
In a year dominated by geopolitical risk and energy shock, currency
investors quickly moved beyond headline trading and increasingly
looked to short-term rate diﬀerentials as the primary guide for
currencies. We expect that to remain the case next year.
Central bank tightening is moving beyond its “insurance” phase,
requiring evidence that inﬂationary pressures are becoming

persistent. Oil prices will remain part of the story, but we think the
more important distinction is between economies where the shock
can generate more lasting inﬂation and those where it cannot. On
top of that, the central banks’ FX-speciﬁc approach will be key.
Remember how, in 2022-2024, the fear of ampliﬁed inﬂation
eﬀects from a weak currency prompted tighter ﬁscal policies
among some often dovish-leaning central banks.
Here are our top picks among G10 currencies for next year:

1. JPY – the best risk/reward. We don’t have a huge
outperformance of the yen in our forecasts, especially relative
to the euro. But that’s primarily due to our assumption that the
euro will drop its French-related risk premium by end-2028, and
Your privacy is important
At ING we respect your right to choose how your data is used.
You can review and change your preferences at any time in
our Cookie policy.
Essential
Performance Cookies
Targeting Cookies

### Page 4

a carry-favourable environment can somewhat contain yen
gains. However, we think the yen oﬀers the best risk-reward
proﬁle in G10. Alongside being sharply undervalued based on
long-term fundamentals, it faces limited risks of dovish
repricing as the Bank of Japan’s tightening cycle predated the
Iran war. It incidentally has strong safe-haven value against
some fat-tail risks (AI bust, ﬁscal dominance, dollar
debasement, some geopolitical events) and stands to be a

beneﬁciary of an ultimate capitulation in USD rates. Downside
risks are, crucially, limited by a coordinated Tokyo-Washington
eﬀort to prevent sharp USD/JPY rallies.
2. CHF – SNB turning pro-franc. If 2027 is the year when global
interest rates peak and then reverse, the opportunity cost of
holding the franc starts to fall. We also think the Swiss National
Bank will prove more tolerant of franc strength. Not only are
there fewer complaints about the strong franc from local

corporates, but Switzerland’s inﬂation is much lower than
trading partners and the SNB might again opt for nominal
franc appreciation to keep the real exchange rate stable.
3. NZD – The high-beta gamble. Among higher-beta currencies,
we see good potential for the New Zealand dollar next year. The
Reserve Bank of New Zealand has delivered a few dovish
surprises, but we think it is underestimating the inﬂation risks
for 2027: we expect at least two hikes, also helped by a fairly

robust growth backdrop. NZD should be favoured against the
Australian dollar to play a decline in global rates, as the
Reserve Bank of Australia may already shift to cuts in the third
quarter of 2027.
Sterling, dollar and AUD face largest dovish
repricing risk

### Page 5

Source: ING, Reﬁnitiv
Dollar decline is delayed, not cancelled
Our baseline for global inﬂation in 2027 remains benign, leaving
most of our major central bank calls more dovish than current
market pricing. We expect the Federal Reserve to deliver one ﬁnal
hike in December 2026, taking rates to a 4.25% peak, but we don’t
think the additional hikes priced by 3Q27 will materialise. Modest
second-round eﬀects should fail to sustain inﬂation once the 2Q27

base-eﬀect decline comes through. Importantly, and unlike in other
economies, we think benign inﬂation rather than weaker growth
will be the main catalyst for a dovish Fed turn. That means markets
may be reluctant to price a large Fed cutting cycle too early, but it’s
equally an environment where we envision a Fed cut in 4Q27.
Ultimately, we expect a nearly 100bp drop in the 2-year USD swap
rate by the end of 2027, with most of the move concentrated in the

year’s second half.
We expect the dollar to weaken alongside that repricing, with
greater conviction in the downside story later in the year. The Fed
should remain cautious about turning dovish before clearer
evidence of disinﬂation emerges, while elevated funding rates will
continue to make it expensive to sell or hedge dollars until some
easing is priced in. And because we also hold dovish views on other
central banks, we don’t expect narrower USD short-term rate

diﬀerentials to trigger a particularly large dollar downturn. We
target a roughly 5% decline in DXY to 97 by end-2027.

### Page 6

Front-end rates to drive DXY lower
Source: ING, Reﬁnitiv
Bond premium on the euro needs bad
news fuel
Our colleagues in the macro and rates team remain cautious about
a recovery in French sentiment in the next couple of quarters. Weak
growth (0.5% in 2027) should continue to make ﬁscal arithmetic
complicated, and we forecast a deﬁcit of 5.5% next year –
assuming a budget is passed – above the 5.0% government target.
Election frontrunner Marine Le Pen has signalled large spending

cuts, although their credibility remains in question. She is, anyway,
a more market-friendly candidate than left-wing rival Jean-Luc
Mélenchon.
We think markets will be wary of jumping into a meaningful OAT
relief rally before the presidential election (18 April-2 May) and likely
parliamentary elections in the summer are out of the way. We are
not forecasting another big selloﬀ in French bonds but think 125-
150bp will be the norm for the OAT-Bund 10yr spread into 2Q27,

before some tightening towards 100bp in 2H27.
The euro has been embedding a French risk premium since early
October, meaning a further material deterioration in sentiment
would probably be needed to generate additional idiosyncratic
pressure. Historically, the link between euro-area sovereign spreads
Your privacy is important
At ING we respect your right to choose how your data is used.
You can review and change your preferences at any time in
our Cookie policy.

Essential
Performance Cookies
Targeting Cookies

### Page 7

and FX has been patchy, with stress episodes often requiring a
steady ﬂow of negative news to sustain the correlation.
We therefore expect French political risk to keep euro sentiment
somewhat subdued into the elections and a subsequent bond-
market relief rally to oﬀer some support, but not to displace central
bank expectations as the main driver of EUR crosses next year. In
other words, we really need to see a signiﬁcant deterioration in the
French situation, prompting ECB bond market intervention, for

EUR/USD to sustain a move below 1.10 through 2027.
EUR already embeds French risk premium
Source: ING, Reﬁnitiv
Central Europe: Same shocks, different
paths for local FX
The Central Europe region is entering a year of divergence. The
Czech Republic, Poland and Hungary face similar risks from energy
prices, geopolitics and volatile global markets, but increasingly
diﬀerent monetary, ﬁscal and growth dynamics mean CEE3 is
becoming less of a single FX trade.

In the Czech Republic, the Czech National Bank has already
delivered two hikes, taking rates to 4%, and remains the most
hawkish central bank in the region. If inﬂation proves more
persistent, further tightening remains possible. Positive nominal
and real rate diﬀerentials and high credibility should therefore keep
the koruna relatively defensive during periods of global uncertainty,
Your privacy is important
At ING we respect your right to choose how your data is used.

You can review and change your preferences at any time in
our Cookie policy.
Essential
Performance Cookies
Targeting Cookies

### Page 8

while any easing of external risks could put it back on an
appreciation path.
Poland is moving diﬀerently. The National Bank of Poland is still
waiting to deliver its ﬁrst hike, leaving the zloty with less monetary
support for now. Strong growth and sizeable EU inﬂows remain
important anchors, but narrower rate diﬀerentials, loose ﬁscal
policy and rising political and rating risks have weakened PLN’s
relative appeal. We therefore see the zloty as more vulnerable to

higher global yields, energy prices and geopolitical risks.
Hungary is heading in the opposite direction, with the National
Bank of Hungary having cut rates in recent months. We expect
another cut only later next year, provided global conditions allow it.
Attractive carry, EU-fund inﬂows and an improving external balance
should support the forint, while progress towards euro adoption
provides another positive longer-term story. The central bank’s

focus on the exchange rate should meanwhile limit the pace of
easing and keep EUR/HUF biased towards the stronger end of its
range.
Divergent central bank responses to the
same shock (%)
Source: ING, Macrobond
Asia: Rotation underway, tech currencies
should lead
Your privacy is important

### Page 9

A softer dollar should provide a broad tailwind for Asian FX,
particularly in the second half of 2027, but we expect performance
to remain highly diﬀerentiated. We favour the Korean won and
Taiwanese dollar, expect further renminbi appreciation – albeit with
greater two-way volatility – and see continued downside risks for
the Indonesian rupiah and Philippine peso. We are more
constructive on the Indian rupee.
Current account strength should continue to favour the tech

currencies. Although the AI cycle may be plateauing, activity
remains high and strong export revenues should support the KRW
and TWD. Exporter FX conversion and measures encouraging the
repatriation of overseas assets should provide additional support.
CNY should also beneﬁt from strong external balances, although
we expect a less one-directional appreciation trend.
Rate support will become less favourable. Most Asian central banks
are further advanced in their tightening cycles than the Fed and

should ﬁnish hiking by early 2027 before preparing for easing.
Narrowing rate support should particularly weigh on the rate-
sensitive IDR. Continued household and corporate demand for
foreign assets could also oﬀset some of the beneﬁts from strong
current accounts.
Lower oil and food prices should help importers in the second half
of 2027, but capital ﬂows are unlikely to recover evenly. Political
uncertainty and the MSCI downgrade should also remain

headwinds for the IDR, while weaker FDI inﬂows leave the PHP
vulnerable. By contrast, we favour the INR, where resilient growth,
supply-chain diversiﬁcation and continued FDI should provide
support, while leaving India less exposed to swings in the AI cycle.
Asia: change in current account balance and
local currency (2Q26 vs 2Q25)

### Page 10

Source: ING, Macrobond
Latam: Banxico’s neglect of the peso
stands out
We’ve said that central bank policy is going to be more important
than ever in 2027. And exchange rates can be an important part of
the monetary toolkit – especially in small, open economies. In a
world where central bank tightening cycles are being priced more
hawkishly, Banxico’s unabashedly dovish stance stands out.
When cutting rates to 6.25% in November, Banxico noted that,

because of slack economic conditions, the pass-through of
exchange rate ﬂuctuations to consumer prices would be limited.
The IMF puts that economic ‘slack’ – or the negative output gap – at
0.4% of GDP in 2027. That makes Banxico far less concerned about
a weaker currency than most other central banks.
In addition, Banxico has now cut the peso’s policy rate protection to
just 225bp over the Fed. That is a record low and leaves the peso
vulnerable to any deterioration in the external environment. We see

the peso as a relative underperformer in 2027.
Further south, the Brazilian real has been causing a lot of pain to
investors recently. The fear is that President Luiz Inácio Lula da
Silva’s pre-election giveaways will come home to roost next year.
His choice of Finance Minister is going to be important, as will any
progress on a 2027 budget. Preventing a bigger sell-oﬀ in the real,
however, is the independent central bank running very high real

Editorial 
Increasing signs of a bottom 
Strategy summary: Democrats’ thumping win in the US mid-
term elections has three key implications: i) policy gridlock is 
back in Washington, likely stalling fiscal expansion and new 
tariffs; ii) the chance of a prolonged Middle East conflict is 
curtailed, lowering oil prices and inflation expectations; and iii) 
the pace of AI data-centre buildout is at risk, curbing AI-linked 
bond supply. Higher corporate and wealth taxes are a long-term 
risk if Democrats win the 2028 presidential elections.   
For portfolios, the election reinforces our mid-cycle playbook 
already in place: remaining broadly balanced across assets, 
while looking for tactical opportunities. As the S&P500 volatility 
index (VIX) surpasses 25, we see rising signs of equity market 
bottoming. We add an opportunistic idea on the US IT sector 
(see page 4), while using elevated volatility to earn income 
through structured strategies. In bonds, we prefer the 3-7-year 
part of the Developed Market bond yield curve and BBB-to-BB-
rated corporate bonds to earn attractive income as AI supply 
eases. In FX, we position for a modestly weaker USD over 6-
12 months by allocating to ex-US assets.  
Policy implications: Oil fell 12% over the past week as 
Democrats captured the US Senate and House. The party also 
flipped governorships in five states – Georgia, Iowa, Nevada, 
Ohio and Texas – securing a majority of governorships among 
50 US states for the first time since 2010. Vice President Vance 
and Republican leaders attributed the defeat to the Iran conflict, 
making it harder for President Trump to sustain it. Democrat 
control of the defence budget from next year further constrains 
conflict escalation. Despite near-term escalation risks, we see 
the geopolitical risk premium in oil prices fading, with oil easing 
towards USD 80/bbl over 6-12 months. Oil-driven disinflation 
should allow central banks to adopt less aggressive policies. 
Adding tactical exposure to US IT sector: Semiconductors 
slumped this week amid concerns over data-centre moratoria, 
while beneficiaries of Democrat policies include defensives, 
affordability-related stocks, tariff rollback beneficiaries and the 
healthcare sector. We use the volatility to add tactical exposure 
to the US IT sector. As we enter mid-cycle dynamics, equities 
should return 8-10% annually. The 2015–2017 mid-cycle offers 
a useful comparison: with an opposition Congress, S&P 500 
returns slowed to 8.3%, but remained positive. Diversification 
across sectors and regions remains a priority amid the rotation. 
Prefer belly of the bond curve: Government bonds rallied on 
expectations of slower growth. We still expect two more Fed 
hikes by H1 2027, given sticky near-term inflation and a robust 
economy. However, cheaper oil should lower inflation towards 
the Fed’s 2% target next year, allowing rate cuts in H2 2027. 
Ultra-long yields remain elevated due to fiscal challenges. 
Bonds, particularly those from non-hyperscaler investment-
grade corporates, offer attractive income. The 3-7-year 
segment – the belly of the yield curve – offers the best balance 
of income and sensitivity to interest-rate changes. 
Credit to benefit from restrained AI supply: Since states 
control data centre regulations, Democrat wins in governorship 
races in leading data centre states, such as Texas and Georgia, 
means growth in this segment is likely to be restricted. Slower 
data-centre construction could improve credit supply-demand 
conditions due to reduced issuance by hyperscalers. However, 
some data-centre-focused deals could become less viable, 
warranting caution towards lower-quality issuers. High-yield 
credit spreads have widened, but BB- and B-rated bonds have 
held up, while CCC-rated debt has reached distressed levels. 
We prefer the BBB–BB range, spanning the boundary between 
investment-grade and high-yield debt, for income. 
A modest headwind for the USD, tailwind for gold: The 
dollar should remain range-bound near term before weakening 
modestly over 6-12 months. Democrat control of the Congress 
limits tariff escalation, fiscal expansion and rapid AI buildout, 
three pillars of US exceptionalism supporting the USD through 
2026. Disinflation should also encourage a less aggressive Fed 
stance, removing another source of USD support. Near-term 
support for the USD index (DXY) remains at 100.7. A weaker-
than-expected payrolls report tonight could accelerate the 
USD’s decline, and vice versa. A softer dollar and sustained 
central-bank buying, led by China, remain supportive of gold.  
—  Rajat Bhattacharya

### Page 4

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 6 November 2026

Top client questions 
  What are the equity market implications of the US mid-
term election results?   
Our view: The US mid-term election outcome adds political 
headwinds but does not alter our outlook for positive equity 
returns. Investors should maintain a Core allocation to global 
equities and continue to diversify their equity exposure across 
regions and sectors. We initiate an opportunistic idea on the US 
information technology (IT) sector, moving our opportunistic 
idea positioning from a defensive tilt to a more balanced 
positioning. Higher volatility also presents opportunities to use 
structured strategies for equity exposure.  
Rationale: The Democrats’ victory in both chambers of Congress 
has supported the outperformance of themes aligned with their 
policy priorities, with clean energy stocks, including solar 
companies, among the notable beneficiaries. Affordability-related 
themes have also benefited, including companies that could gain 
from potential tariff rollbacks. Medicaid-related stocks have also 
risen on hopes that healthcare will gain from policy changes. 
Meanwhile, semiconductor stocks remain under pressure amid 
concerns about potential moratoriums on data centres. Our AI capital 
expenditure (capex) estimates remain conservative relative to 
consensus, but with a potential reset in expectations around data 
centre spending, investors should remain selective.  
We initiate an opportunistic idea on the US IT sector after a 
significant 17% correction this quarter. Historically, this has been 
followed by strong positive returns over the next few quarters (see 
table). Semiconductors, about 40% of US IT, appears oversold after 
correcting more than 30% since we closed our opportunistic idea a 
month ago. Earnings growth for US IT continues to be solid, with 
volume driven growth in semiconductors, AI monetisation 
opportunities, ongoing cloud growth and resilient services revenue.  
As we highlighted in our November 2026 Global Market Outlook, 
the transition to mid-cycle is typically characterised by greater 
political headwinds. The mid-term election outcome reinforces this 
trend. The mid-cycle experience of 2015-17 offers a useful historical 
parallel for returns that moderate, but remain positive. Although we 
trimmed global equities to a Core allocation last week, we continue 
to expect equities to deliver mid-to-high single-digit returns per 
annum during the mid-cycle, even amid political headwinds, 
supported by steady earnings growth in both 2027 and 2028. 
Stay invested and diversify, with Overweight allocations to the US 
and AxJ, which offer the strongest earnings growth. Sector 
diversification is key as we expect market leadership to broaden. We 
are Overweight the US IT, US healthcare, US financial, US materials, 
Europe ex-UK financial and Japan financial sectors. 
—  Fook Hien Yap, Senior Investment Strategist

Policy uncertainty and a change in the innovation 
cycle is consistent with our view of a mid-cycle 
transition, where equity returns moderate but 
remain positive

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 6 November 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What are the key takeaways from Q3 2026 US earnings? 
Our view: Q3 US earnings remain robust, but more modest 
earnings beats reinforce our view that equity markets are 
entering a mid-cycle phase. We remain Overweight US equities, 
but recommend investors to maintain diversified sector 
exposure as returns moderate and market leadership broadens. 
Rationale: As of 30 October, 82% of S&P500 companies that had 
reported had beaten earnings estimates. However, aggregate 
earnings were just 4.5% above expectations, a smaller beat than in 
recent quarters. Still, Q3 US earnings growth remains robust at 
31.6% y/y, supporting expected full-year growth of 36.1% in 2026 
and 16.4% in 2027 for the S&P500. The slower pace of upgrades 
suggests the bull market is maturing as investors focus on the 
sustainability of earnings growth. This raises the hurdle for positive 
share price reactions and makes selectivity more essential. For Big 
Tech, capex commitments remain high, while free cash flow is under 
pressure and expected to remain negative. Financing risks weigh on 
sentiment, particularly for companies with weaker balance sheets.  
We remain Overweight US equities, given the strong earnings 
growth, but we expect elevated volatility and modest returns. We 
prefer quality exposure within technology while maintaining 
diversified exposure across US financials, healthcare and materials. 
—  Ryan Goh, Investment Strategist 
  What to expect from Japanese banks’ upcoming results?   
Our view: We expect Japanese banks’ earnings growth to 
continue over the medium term, supported by higher domestic 
net interest income (NII) and resilient fee income. We remain 
positive on the sector, favouring banks with strong deposit 
franchises and scope to increase shareholder returns. 
Investors will focus on whether full-year guidance upgrades 
and buybacks exceed expectations. 
Rationale: The BoJ’s September rate hike to 1.25% took effect late 
in the quarter, so its benefit should be more visible from the 
December quarter as loans reprice. We expect three further BoJ 
rate hikes by June 2027, supporting further NII expansion. We 
will also look for signs that stronger corporate investment is driving 
faster loan growth and higher financing and advisory fees.  
We prefer to place more weight on guidance upgrades driven by 
recurring lending and fee income than on those driven by gains on 
equity disposals. Continued reductions in cross-shareholdings 
could free up capital for additional buybacks, providing a further 
boost to return on equity (ROE). Key risks: faster-than-expected 
deposit rate repricing, rising credit costs and bond portfolio losses. 
—  Robin Xie, CFA, Senior Investment Strategist

### Page 6

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 6 November 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What are the fundamental reasons behind your India 
equity upgrade? Which sectors offer favourable risk-reward? 
Our View: Indian equities offer an increasingly attractive risk-
reward profile. With India’s valuation premium to Emerging 
Markets (EMs) narrowing, foreign ownership near decade lows, 
Underweight EM positioning and a stabilising Indian rupee 
(INR), conditions are increasingly supportive of Indian equity 
outperformance vs. the broader AxJ region.  
Rationale: We have upgraded Indian equities to Overweight within 
our AxJ equities allocation. While Indian markets have witnessed 
USD 32bn of foreign investor outflows in 2026 year-to-date, strong 
domestic institutional investor inflows of USD 74bn have more 
than offset foreign selling. These outflows and the Indian equity 
market’s underperformance vs. global peers reflect broader EM 
allocation shifts towards AI, rather than weakening Indian 
fundamentals. Capital has gravitated towards markets such as the 
US, Taiwan and South Korea, which offer direct exposure to AI 
beneficiaries. However, we expect market leadership to broaden, as 
the rapid AI buildout is increasingly debt-funded, and US mid-term 
election results raise uncertainty. We prefer to diversify and we see 
investors reallocating towards structurally strong growth markets 
such as India, which offer non-AI driven growth.  
The RBI’s measures have supported INR stability, but underlying 
USD demand remains elevated. Lower oil prices would improve 
external balances, but the INR is likely to follow a path of gradual, 
controlled depreciation rather than a quick downward move. On 
valuation to its own historical average, the Nifty 50 one-year forward 
P/E is trading 18% below its long-term average while ROE at 14.7% 
is above historical averages. With India now marginally Underweight 
in large EM portfolios compared to the c.2% average Overweight 
historically, the case for a recovery in foreign flows is 
strengthening. 
India’s earnings growth remains intact. The Nifty 50 clocked Q1 
FY27 and Q2 FY27 profit-after-tax growth of c.20% y/y (ex-oil 
marketing companies). Large-caps offer greater earnings visibility 
and downside protection, while stronger growth in small- and mid-
caps warrants a selective approach given elevated valuations.  
India’s growth outlook remains supported by a strengthening 
investment cycle, manufacturing momentum, supply chain 
diversification, resilient consumption and healthy corporate and 
banking balance sheets. Importantly, earnings growth remains 
robust and broad-based across the financial, metals, telecom, 
automobiles and other domestic sectors, with Nifty 50 earnings 
growth expectations of c.14-15% over 2027 vis a vis AxJ’s 
technology driven earnings growth of 29% in 2027. 
—  Shaily Gang, Chief Investment Strategist, India

### Page 7

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 6 November 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Does the emerging US job market data alter your view of 
the Fed’s rate path and government bond yields? 
Our view: We do not expect the recent and incoming US job 
market data to alter our outlook unless there are significant 
deviations from the US economy’s current trajectory.  
Rationale: The US economy continues to exhibit resilience, with the 
latest Job Openings and Labour Turnover Survey data showing job 
openings surprising to the upside at 7.5mn. Initial jobless claims 
remain near three-month lows. There is little evidence of a material 
slowdown in labour demand. Inflation is expected to trend lower 
through H2 2027, but the disinflationary impulse remains a medium-
term story and is unlikely to manifest meaningfully in the near term. 
The market is now pricing in two additional 25bps hikes by H1 
2027 (down from three previously), in line with our view. These two 
rate hikes are largely priced into the US government bond yield 
curve. We estimate 2- and 10-year US government bond yields to 
range between 4.5-4.75% and 5-5.25%, respectively, over the next 
three months. Our twelve-month forecast for 2- and 10-year US 
government bond yields are 4.25-4.5% and 4.75-5%, respectively, 
The 2-year yield is already trading within our expected range and 
would only move materially higher if a significantly stronger-than-
expected payrolls print led markets to price in a third hike. With two 
hikes baked in, long-run disinflation expectations intact and an 
uncertain fiscal outlook, the longer end of the yield curve is 
expected to be less sensitive to a single payrolls release. 
—  Ray Heung, Senior Investment Strategist 
  What is your outlook for the GBP following the BoE’s rate 
hike this week? Do you see any impact on EUR/GBP? 
Our view: The BoE rate hike supports the GBP, but upside 
remains limited. We remain constructive on EUR/GBP, 
expecting a rebound towards 0.8450. 
Rationale: The BoE hiked by 25bps to 4% as expected. The GBP 
was little changed, despite hawkish inflation guidance. With markets 
pricing only around 50bps of additional BoE tightening over the next 
12 months, we see the GBP remaining vulnerable. In contrast, the 
ECB held its deposit rate at 2.50% in October but signalled readiness 
to tighten if inflation remains elevated. We expect Euro area inflation 
to rise further, keeping the ECB’s tightening bias alive. This suggests 
EUR/GBP has fallen too far relative to interest rate differentials.  
Germany’s planned increase in federal debt issuance should 
support Euro area growth, while Moody’s decision not to downgrade 
France has removed an important EUR tail risk. Meanwhile, tighter 
UK fiscal policy is likely to weigh on domestic growth and business 
investment. We expect EUR/GBP to recover towards 0.8450. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist

Standard Chartered Bank

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Why is the AUD underperforming despite the RBA hike? 
Our view: The AUD has lagged because markets had largely 
priced in the RBA’s November rate hike to 4.85%, and the bank 
gave investors less reason to expect another. Equity weakness 
also weighed on the currency. We remain bullish on AUD/USD 
and would add on pullbacks. Australia’s interest rate advantage 
and a sustained recovery in risk sentiment should support a 
move towards 0.72 in three months and 0.74 in 12 months. 
Rationale: Ahead of the 3 November RBA rate decision, market 
pricing implied an 88% chance of a 25bps hike, leaving little scope 
for an upside surprise. What changed after the announcement 
was the pricing of the next hike. The RBA dropped its reference 
to further rate increases, and three members preferred a rate hold. 
Markets scaled back the odds of rates reaching 5.10% next year to 
around 30%, thus weighing on the AUD. 
The AUD is sensitive to swings in global risk appetite, especially 
the US tech sell-off spread to global equities, triggering margin calls 
and weighing on the currency. An equity recovery would ease that 
pressure. Even after the two 25bps Fed hikes we expect by mid-
2027, Australia would retain a 35bps rate advantage over the top 
of the Fed’s target range, supporting a gradual AUD recovery. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist

We see AUD/USD upside risks on a sustained 
recovery in risk sentiment

Editorial 
Opportunities from the bond sell-off  
Strategy summary: The global bond sell-off is creating tactical 
and medium-term opportunities. In the US, September’s yield 
surge has improved the risk-reward in 10-year government 
bonds, presenting an attractive entry point for investors with a 
6-12-month horizon. We believe bond and money markets are 
overly hawkish about the Fed outlook, given disinflation is likely 
to return as impact of oil prices and tariffs ease next year. 
In equities, we stick to quality, especially sectors riding the AI-
led earnings boom. The upcoming Q3 earnings season is likely 
to show AI-investments are powering sectors beyond the 
technology and communications services, such as financials, 
materials and power and electrification. We remain constructive 
on the semiconductor and memory segments. We also expect 
the US Dollar to peak in the next few weeks along with short-
to-medium-term US bond yields. This creates opportunities for 
AUD corporate bonds, Emerging Market USD bonds and gold. 
Attractive risk-reward in US 10-year government bonds. 
The US 10-year bond yield rose more than 50bps in September 
(biggest monthly rise since 2022) and 115bps this year, amid 
expectations of economic resilience, higher Fed rates, fuel-
driven inflation, AI-related capital demand and fiscal concerns. 
Valuations appear close to fair value, improving the 6-12-month 
risk-reward. Bond arithmetic suggests losses of less than 1% if 
the yield rises to 6%, vs. more than 10% gains if it falls to 4.5%. 
Resilient US economic activity, but softer-than-expected 
inflation. September data showed robust US employment 
(jobless claims, ADP) and core capital-goods orders. However, 
consumer confidence remains depressed, real disposable 
income is flat and households are drawing on savings to sustain 
spending. Against this divided backdrop, core PCE inflation - 
the Fed’s preferred gauge - was softer than expected at 0.2% 
m/m and 3.0% y/y. A revised method to measure PCE inflation 
also indicate that annual core PCE inflation was 35bps lower 
than previously reported, narrowing the gap with CPI inflation. 
Markets appear overly hawkish on the Fed. Money markets 
price about 85bps of tightening over the next 12 months. A 
strong labour market (September’s non-farm payrolls data is 
due tonight) may justify one or two further hikes, but aggressive 
tightening after the sharp rise in bond yields – which has 
already tightened financial conditions - could threaten financial 
stability. US small- and mid-cap equities have already fallen 8–
9% from recent peaks over the past couple of months, 
materially underperforming large caps, as energy and 
borrowing-cost concerns intensify. While some Fed speakers 
remain hawkish, we agree with New York Fed President 
Williams’ that there is no urgency to tighten. We see little 
chance of an October Fed rate hike and expect inflation data 
for Sep-Nov determining whether the Fed moves in December. 
Stay with quality equities and AI beneficiaries. US large 
caps have absorbed the rise in bond yields relatively well, 
supported by expectations of robust earnings growth led by AI-
related investment. We continue to favour quality companies 
and sectors benefiting from this AI cycle. Semiconductors and 
memory remain preferred exposures: structural AI demand 
should extend the memory upcycle, while long-term supply 
agreements provide better revenue visibility. The opportunity is 
also broadening beyond technology and communication 
services into financials, materials, power and electrification. 
Opportunities in AUD corporate bonds and gold. The USD 
rebound alongside higher US yields offers a tactical opportunity 
to add higher-yielding Emerging Market USD and non-USD 
bonds. We remain positive on AUD corporate bonds, which 
offer a yield premium over comparable US credit. Our 
constructive medium-term AUD/USD view could add returns for 
USD-based investors over the next 6–12 months. We would 
also use gold’s recent pullback to add exposure, supported by 
sustained Emerging Market central bank demand. We continue 
to view USD 4,000/oz as important technical support for gold.  
—  Rajat Bhattacharya

### Page 4

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 2 October 2026

Top client questions 
  Do you expect more RBA rate hikes after its less hawkish 
guidance? What is your view on the AUD and AUD bonds? 
Our view: We expect the RBA to leave rates unchanged, but 
retain the option to hike again should inflation intensify. We 
maintain our medium-term constructive AUD view and maintain 
our opportunistically bullish AUD corporate bonds idea. 
Rationale: The RBA’s forward guidance remains data dependent, 
explicitly allowing further tightening if needed as inflation remains too 
high.  At 4.60%, Australia’s cash rate is currently 60–85bp above the 
Fed’s 3.75–4.00% range. Thus, even with our two additional 25bps 
Fed hikes expectation, Australian rates would hold a modest positive 
rate differential. We expect the AUD to strengthen once the Fed 
signals that US rates are near their peak, which should allow resilient 
Australian fundamentals to reassert themselves. Key risks are 
persistently high US yields, weaker China growth or a sharper 
deterioration in global risk sentiment. 
We believe risk-reward has become more attractive for AUD bonds 
following the bear-flattening of the AUD yield curve. Markets are 
already pricing in one more RBA hike through end-2027, presenting 
an attractive opportunity to earn a yield even as increasingly 
restrictive financial conditions reinforce disinflation over time. 
Meanwhile, the positive interest rate differential with the US supports 
the currency outlook, supporting the case for AUD bonds. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
— Cedric Lam, Senior Investment Strategist 
— Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist 
  What does the incoming US data tell us about the path for 
US 10-year bond yields from here? 
Our view: The risk-reward in US 10-year yields has turned 
attractive following their move higher amid incoming US data. 
Rationale: Recent US data has indeed started to show softer-than-
expected inflation. However, market technicals are likely temporarily 
delaying a move lower in bond yields, with reports suggesting the 
rise in yields is creating forced selling amongst hedge funds and real 
estate investment trusts. Nevertheless, we do not expect this to be 
an extended selloff. We have initiated an opportunistic bullish idea 
on US 10-year government bonds this week and continue to believe 
this idea offers an attractive risk/reward on a 6-12-month horizon as 
yields become attractive enough to entice buyer flow to return. On a 
broader allocation basis, we retain our structural preference for 3-7-
year maturities in bonds. 
— Cedric Lam, Senior Investment Strategist

We remain positive on AUD corporate bonds amid 
Australia-US interest rate differentials and positive 
outlook for the AUD

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 2 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  How should investors position in the GBP ahead of the UK 
budget announcement next month? 
Our view: We maintain a medium-term constructive GBP view, 
but keep near-term exposure measured ahead of the 28 October 
Budget. We would view any pre-Budget weakness as an 
opportunity to gradually build GBP exposure. 
Rationale: The government’s decision to adjust the pension triple 
lock from 2030 is an early indication of greater willingness to tackle 
longer-term spending pressures, reinforcing Chancellor Healey’s 
commitment to fiscal discipline and meeting the fiscal rules. Markets 
are likely to scrutinise the Budget closely for evidence that this 
discipline extends to near-term tax and spending decisions. A 
credible Budget should help contain pressure on UK government 
bond yields and support GBP. Any fiscal slippage remains a key risk.  
Near term, we prefer measured GBP exposure as elevated US yields 
and repricing towards further Fed tightening could continue to 
support the USD. Over the medium term, we expect GBP to rebound 
as the Fed eventually signals the peak of its tightening cycle. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist 
  How would different US mid-term election outcome 
scenarios affect the outlook for US equities? 
Our view: We believe election outcomes will have a limited 
impact over the next 12 months. We remain positive on US 
equities driven by fundamental earnings growth.  
Rationale: Over the past 50 years, the ruling US President’s party 
has retained control of both chambers of Congress on only two 
occasions (1978 and 2002). All other elections resulted in an 
opposition sweep or a divided government.  
Based on the latest prediction markets, it appears that an opposition 
sweep (both the House and Senate flips from Republican to 
Democrats) is more likely than a divided government (in which the 
House flips to Democrats, while the Senate remains under 
Republican control). A scenario where Republicans retain a majority 
is least likely per prediction markets. 
While mid-term elections may introduce near-term volatility, we 
caution against overreaction. Historically, the average return for the 
S&P 500 following an opposition sweep is 17% over the next 12 
months, but the average return following a divided government is not 
significantly worse, at 15%. We prefer to base our investment views 
on fundamental earnings growth, where investor attention should 
focus on the upcoming Q3 earnings season. 
—  Jason Wong, Senior Equity Analyst

GBP/USD’s pre-Budget weakness sets an 
opportunity to gradually build GBP exposure over 
the medium-term
